notes: INPUT READY 2026-10-15: T-071 (Qwen, Codex APPROVE r3) holds the bounded owner options O1-O4 for the strict-storage mechanism, each with its preceding evidence and file scopes, in .collab/T-071-storage-namespace-options.md (read it with its Correction r2 and r3 sections). Claude has not read its content (owner rule 2026-10-15, classifier family). The owner's choice of option is what unblocks this card. Owner decision, not a classifier stop and not a test. Codex's readiness record finds that an execution can choose the descriptor of objects it creates, so the current construction cannot promise strict peer exclusion for execution-created descendants. Before S2-G: either choose and evidence a mechanism that removes creation-time authority across all writable surfaces (or confines storage so peers cannot traverse), or record an ADR that relaxes peer exclusion. Codex's handoff says Claude must not implement the creation-permitting design as if this were closed. T-007 (refusals only) and T-010 (the comparison) are what can go ahead now. Evidence collected 2026-10-12 under the owner's request: see T-011 (`.collab/T-004-evidence.md`, findings F1-F5). It does not unblock this card — the decision is the owner's, and the OS-side evidence stays on T-008.

## Coordination update - 2026-10-12 (Codex, owner-authorized)

AUTH-COLLAB-1012: owner asked Codex to update .collab docs, divide next work between Claude and Qwen, and check Qwen's finished work. Owner also explicitly permits preventive assignment to Qwen when Claude is expected to be classifier-blocked. This authorizes coordination and stated read-only planning; it does not authorize new Windows runs, production activation, cleanup or pushes.
Existing code-card authorization AUTH-1012 remains scoped; new implementation cards are proposed until their named scope is authorized.

T-006 authorship stays Claude; owner reports Qwen finished its verification. Codex checked saved logs, not reran tests. Qwen has no continuing T-006 implementation task. Only T-014 is newly assigned to Qwen, as preventive routing requested by owner; no new classifier refusal is asserted.

Inspect exactly these commits with `git show --stat <sha>` and `git show <sha>`: 3489a39 8997446
Read every document named under refs and judge the change against them and against `accept`.

Look for, in this order: violations of the canonical design, ADRs or gate items; scope creep beyond the card; correctness bugs; missing or vacuous tests for the acceptance criteria; evidence claims stronger than what was run. Skip style nits.

Reply in exactly this format, nothing before it:
VERDICT: APPROVE | CHANGES | ESCALATE
1. path:line - problem - required change
(one numbered line per finding; "none" if APPROVE with no findings)
Not checked: <what you could not verify, or "nothing">

ESCALATE means only the owner can decide (canonical-doc conflict, missing authorization).

Used by _integration_facts (begin_merge guard), by record_merge (Refused in its transaction for either outcome before any write), by worker._merge (Refused before any node call, PENDING and MERGING alike) and by the planner, which no longer plans a merge whose revision is None. review: —
notes: CLOSED 2026-10-19 by the owner (verbatim): "T-093, T-099, T-075, T-077, T-079 and T-005 close these". DONE 2026-10-14: .collab/T-077-long-home.md. T-075's guess corrected - nothing is swallowed and verification never starts: git worktree add refuses the task worktree (LocalNode.add_worktree local.py:373-377 raises OSError with Git's text; same pattern mission_worktree :463-466); manager.py:222-226 records a CHARGED workspace refusal ('no workspace: ...'), every attempt is used, task fails as execution, the mission spends 2 real planner calls replanning, then BLOCKED 'no replan left'; 'Core went idle' is only the rig's message (test_verification.py:57). Two limits: (1) the worktree's .git path must be <= 220 chars ('fatal: $GIT_DIR too big'), worktree = home/worktrees/prj_.../msn_.../t1 so home + 80 -> ARCHEUS_HOME cap 140; core.longpaths does NOT lift it; default home %LOCALAPPDATA%\Archeus is 34 chars here, so users reach it only with a deep explicit ARCHEUS_HOME; (2) a tracked file whose checkout path passes 259 chars fails checkout - with the default home any repo path over ~149 chars, REAL USERS CAN HIT THIS, unless their own git config sets core.longpaths=true (which lifts it). Real defect is the classification: a deterministic refusal travels the charged, retryable workspace path (T-049 already flagged that path as interim). Proposed fix card with owner options A (pre-check .git length > 220 -> new uncharged stop_reason e.g. path_too_long naming path/length/limit/fix), B (-c core.longpaths=true on Core's worktree add/remove; open question whether the harness/user git inside the worktree cope over 260), C (shorter internal worktree names, Core-owned layout change, needs its own decision), D (deterministic Git refusals uncharged in general). K1/K2 store and seed paths not measured (K2 _Manifest refuses > 259 by design). OWNER 2026-10-14 in the Claude session (AskUserQuestion answer): "Start investigation (Recommended)" - test runs and reading only; the note names the failing file:line and the swallowed exception; any fix needs the owner's approval of its scope. CLAIMED 2026-10-14 (Claude fork). Drafted by Claude from T-075. Needs owner authorization. Real users can have long ARCHEUS_HOME paths (LOCALAPPDATA under a long profile name), so this may be a product bug, not only a test-environment one.

DONE e3c375e + ada596c: STOP_REASONS and UNCHARGED gain path_too_long; executions.BLOCKING = ('path_too_long',); refuse(..., missions=None) calls the existing _block after _respond in the same command with the refusal's reason (no second refusal path); the task returns READY uncharged but the engine dispatches only while the mission is EXECUTING (engine._step), so nothing loops until the user resumes; manager._spawn catches PathTooLong before OSError -> refuse(stop_reason='path_too_long', missions=self.missions) (manager.py was already in the card's original scope); worker._merge: PathTooLong from mission_worktree logs a warning, blocks an EXECUTING mission via missions.fire(trigger='block', reason='no mission worktree: ...'), and plan() stops planning that mission's merges until its state changes; present.ts STOPPED.path_too_long = 'stopped: its workspace path is too long for Git; use a shorter Archeus folder'. GAPS: the app label never shows for this refusal - it happens before the process exists, so the execution ends ABANDONED and endReason says 'it never started' for any stop reason (STOPPED read only for killed); the block reason is visible on the mission's state_changed event; changing ABANDONED rendering would affect every abandoned refusal, left alone; the merge-time hold is in memory (ponytail: comment in worker.py) - after a Core restart one more refused merge, no re-block since already BLOCKED; a merge in a non-EXECUTING mission cannot be blocked (block leaves only EXECUTING), held with a log warning only, not reached in tests; resume-after-unblock cycle untested; tools inside the worktree vs paths over 260 untested. PARTIAL e3c375e (B + the in-scope half of A): one _WORKTREE = ('-c', 'core.longpaths=true', 'worktree') used by add_worktree, mission_worktree's add and remove_worktree; a 270-char tracked file under a 109-char root checks out and is removed, repo .git/config and global config byte-identical afterwards. check_worktree_path(path) raises PathTooLong(OSError) when len(<path>/.git) > GIT_DIR_MAX (220 on Windows, None elsewhere), after the restart early-return and before makedirs or any Git, in add_worktree and both branches of mission_worktree, measuring the actual path; at home 135 the task worktree (.git 215) is made while the mission worktree from worker._mission_wt (.git 221) is refused with its path, nothing created, no branch. STILL CHARGED until the extension lands: path_too_long is not in STOP_REASONS (entities.py:867, validated via _CHOICES :958; test_execution_units.py:225 asserts set(STOP_REASONS) - set(UNCHARGED) == {'workspace'}); refuse()/_respond() (executions.py:140, :371-374) return an uncharged refusal to READY -> endless loop unless the mission is blocked in the same command (_block executions.py:433); worker._merge (worker.py:296) -> mission_worktree, pass_once (:84-95) catches only Refused/lost-race, so PathTooLong escapes and the merge retries every pass; present.ts STOPPED has no label. Open: tools inside the worktree vs paths over 260 (they read the user's config). IN PROGRESS: second commit under the scope extension (Claude fork). CLAIMED 2026-10-14 (Claude fork). Open question carried from T-077 option B: whether tools later run inside the worktree (the harness, the user's own git) cope with paths over 260 - they read the user's config, not Core's -c; not tested here.

### T-092 · Multi-task merge-back: the plan digest covers runtime merge fields; git_snapshot fails on an autocrlf phantom
owner: claude · status: review
auth: Owner 2026-10-15 in the Claude session (AskUserQuestion answer, verbatim): "Authorize T-092 (Recommended)" - fork fixes F1 (hash merge fields at defaults so old digests stay valid) and F2, mutation-verified; T-080's multi-task test lands after; local commits only; independent Claude reviewer.
scope: AS BUILT, detached checkout D:/archeus-pv20-digest from the T-072 candidate 91cbb0c: archeus/core/planning/validate.py (digest), archeus/node/local.py (git_snapshot), tests/v1/unit/test_planning_units.py, tests/v1/integration/test_verification.py. worker.py and application/verification.py unchanged.
refs: T-080 fork findings 2026-10-15 (notes on T-080; draft test + debug diff at scratchpad c71a9b1f/t080_debug.diff); validate.digest docstring ("every task's contract"); P13 state-machines section 13 (integration_state, merged_revision); P19 B4 precedent for a field added to a digested entity (edited_by).
accept: (F1) a task's merge-back (integration_state, merged_revision, conflicts) does not change the plan digest, and a digest stored before the fix still verifies - the runtime fields are hashed at their defaults, not dropped (old digests included them as null/[]); a two-task worktree mission dispatches the second task after the first merges and completes. (F2) a task already MERGED is not verified again when a later task is; and/or a worktree whose `git status` shows a modification that `git diff` does not (core.autocrlf=true) does not fail git_snapshot with "nothing to commit". Root cause named for each, mutation-verified. Then T-080's multi-task test lands on top.
verify: RAN 2026-10-15 (Claude fork) on 2ff46ce: unit test_planning_units + test_domain + test_paths + test_execution_units + test_repo_store + test_mutation_suites_parse, integration test_verification + test_long_paths + test_planning + test_execution (-m "not real_process") 1192 passed; tests/v1/judge + unit test_policy_units + test_policy_boundaries + integration test_plan_edit + test_policy + test_chat_approve 302 passed, 10 skipped, 2 xfailed (a first judge+policy run had 1 failure, test_C5_two_sessions_on_one_mission_see_each_others_changes[http]: passed 3 of 3 alone, its file 8 of 8 and the whole set on rerun - recorded as a load flake, cause not found); final on HEAD test_planning_units + test_verification 110 passed; ruff . clean; diff --check clean; no CR in the blobs. Mutants: F1 old validate.py -> the digest unit test and the multi-task test both fail; F1 "drop the keys" -> the unit test fails (old digests break); F2 old git_snapshot -> the snapshot test fails with "nothing to commit, working tree clean". Basetemps D:/t092* deleted. NOT RUN: full tests/v1, POSIX, e2e.
commits: (detached checkout D:/archeus-pv20-digest on the T-072 candidate 91cbb0c) 7701f2e (F1), 1da0f7d (F2), 2ff46ce (T-080 multi-task test), 44cbb47
review: reviews/T-092-2ff46ce.claude.md APPROVE (independent Claude reviewer, 2026-10-15; 5 non-blocking findings); Codex canonical batch for digest compatibility still queued
notes: HARDENED 44cbb47 (independent review findings 1-2, tests only): every task in the both-layouts test leaves its own file, so the earlier-layout task's merge into _mission is asserted (product mutant "earlier-layout task recorded MERGED without merging" passes 2ff46ce's test, fails this one); new test: a failing `diff --cached --quiet` (128) still commits the staged work (mutant "error counts as nothing staged" caught); test_verification.py 69 passed, ruff clean. REVIEW 2026-10-15 APPROVE. Corrections to this card: the title's and T-080's earlier "a MERGED task is re-verified" diagnosis is WITHDRAWN (the phantom belonged to plan v2's t1, confirmed by the reviewer's probe); the worker.py:292 open item is NOT real for worktree tasks (git add -A clears the phantom first, checked on git 2.49) and is at most a question for in-place tasks whose verification command rewrites tracked files; 2ff46ce's message gives the wrong reason for every task writing the fix (only t2 needs it: t1/t2 dispatch together from the fixture base, t3 starts from the mission branch). Hardening taken as a follow-up commit: t1 writes its own marker asserted in _mission; local.py:539 fail-closed branch pinned (a diff --cached error must not read as nothing staged - that mutant survived). DONE 7701f2e + 1da0f7d + 2ff46ce. F1: validate.digest resets integration_state/merged_revision/conflicts to their defaults before hashing (_TASK_RUNTIME), so an approval-time digest still holds after a merge and every stored digest stays valid; all digest checks (authorization.intact, guards.ready, the execution binding) go through it; the unit test also asserts every Task field outside _FROZEN is skipped or reset. F2 CORRECTED DIAGNOSIS: no MERGED task is re-verified - the second "t1" was the new t1 of a REPLAN (plan v2: in the first fork's test t2 lacked t1's fix, failed twice, and the mission replanned), rewriting calc.py with content its mission branch already held. Root cause: under core.autocrlf the checkout holds CRLF, the LF rewrite changes the size, `git status` reports M without comparing content while `add -A` stages nothing, and git_snapshot's commit raised an OSError out of the verification pass; git_snapshot now returns the unchanged HEAD when `diff --cached --quiet` finds nothing staged (verified as "the task changed nothing"). OPEN, not in scope: worker.py:293 git_dirty(tracked_only) can read the same phantom as a moved revision (a re-verification, not a crash). T-080's test needed each task to carry the fix (each is verified in its own worktree from the mission base). FOUND 2026-10-15 by the T-080 correction fork (Codex batch finding on T-080 asked for a mission with task worktrees in both layouts). F1 (certain, code-read by Claude too): validate._TASK_SKIP = ('id','state','failure_class','plan_id') leaves integration_state/merged_revision/conflicts (entities.py:926-928) in the digest; the first merge-back changes it, authorization.py:519-523 then refuses every later dispatch ("plan v1 does not match its recorded digest") and the mission ends BLOCKED. Reproduces with no legacy worktree. F2 (seen only with F1 patched in the test): after t2 verifies, t1 (already MERGED) is verified again; its worktree reads ` M calc.py` with an empty `git diff` (core.autocrlf=true), so worker.py:185-187 calls git_snapshot, which fails at local.py:446 "nothing to commit, working tree clean". Implementation card inside the accepted P13 design -> independent Claude reviewer; digest compatibility is the one point Codex may want to see.

### Decisions taken - 2026-10-15 (owner): P20 order after slice F, D12, D13, D14

Owner 2026-10-15 ~00:40 in the Claude session (AskUserQuestion answers, verbatim): order "I then H, beside G (Recommended)" - slice I first, then H, both while G (PV-20) waits on T-004, each slice with its own commits and gate items; D12 "Stdlib harness (Recommended)" - random.Random(seed) + recorded corpus + structured mutation in tools/fuzz_p20.py, fixed seed and small budget in CI, large on demand, no new dependency; D13 + D14 "Approve both as written (Recommended)" - the drills and mutant ids exactly as p20-design-gate.md section 4 states them. notes: PUSHED 2026-10-15 on the owner's answer "Push now (Recommended)": b26225a..82c7e2d; CI run pending. DONE 82c7e2d (tests only: tests/v1/e2e/motion.py, test_spa_p16.py, test_spa_motion.py, test_spa_strip.py). CAUSE: freeze() installed Playwright's clock only when a test froze, and started tracking reads only then. Every timer and frame the app asked for earlier stayed NATIVE: a stream frame arriving just before freeze left the cache's 50 ms re-read timer (data/cache.ts invalidate) native, so it fired in real time after settle() had returned, its read was untracked, and the answer redrew the map after f0. Platform-independent; CI's faster Linux run hits the window more often. FIX: motion.watch() installs the clock and tracks reads before the page loads (_open calls it); freeze() only pauses. SECOND DEFECT found: freeze passed ms to Python's pause_at, which takes SECONDS (int(time*1000)), so every frozen test jumped the page clock ~57,000 years; it now pauses 1 s ahead as intended. test_spa_strip's orbit check had silently relied on that jump to clear ORBIT_EVERY_MS (5 s) after the attempt's own turn; it now advances EVERY_MS explicitly. No product change; no timeout raised, no retry added. Push needs the owner.

CORRECTION 2d2f3b0 + e7141c1 (2026-10-15, Claude fork): (1) in memory mode a token an earlier shell stored is revoked THROUGH ITSELF before it is deleted (GET /v1/sync for its device id, POST /v1/devices/{id}/revoke with an idempotency key, then the key is deleted); 401 = already dead, deleted with no revoke; Core down or refusing = kept, retried next launch (never delete the only copy of a live admin token). Byte erasure NOT achieved and not claimed: LevelDB keeps a deleted value until it compacts and no page can force it, so what may remain is a DEAD token (the reviewer's own bar). deleteDatabase of both archeus and archeus-snapshot was built and probed: it erased the bytes in a standalone QtWebEngine probe, but in the real shell flow both deletes reported success and the bytes survived the next launch, and it costs B7's snapshot - so it was dropped. REVIEW CHANGES: (1) in memory mode, before deleting a stored token, the page revokes its own device (GET /v1/sync for its id, then POST /v1/devices/{id}/revoke), node test; the deleted value stays in the IndexedDB log after delete (real QtWebEngine probe) - erase it (compact/clear the store, or delete the IndexedDB database) after revocation; (2) docs: the section 5.1 amendment and p20 items 109/111 describe the old behaviour; no section 12 item records the desktop shell page slot, the reload behaviour, or that a second concurrent archeus gui signs out the first window. FIXING 2026-10-15 (Claude fork). DONE a2be0f4 + 9abca6c. I-F1: a broad principal that may READ the home or run/local-token now warns too ("ARCHEUS_HOME <path> can be read by <name> (<SID>), so any signed-in user of this machine can read Core's full-scope local token; move ARCHEUS_HOME ..."); write is still reported first; warn only, no ACL write. I-F2 design choice: off-the-record was REJECTED because the same profile holds the appearance choice and P19 B7's offline snapshot across restarts. Instead the shell's page takes slot "desktop shell page" (each page credential revokes the previous one) and keeps its token in memory only (memory=1 -> sessionStorage flag, module variable, IndexedDB never written, a token an earlier shell left is deleted); a reload in the shell is answered with a fresh launch address (?r=N forces a real load); Core down -> plain reload -> unauthenticated probe -> B7 snapshot. `core --open` and the legacy GUI's "Open Archeus V1" are unchanged (no slot, token persisted in that browser as before). FINDINGS (recorded, not changed here): (a) the page token is still minted with observe+control+approve+admin and no expiry (cli/main.py _launch_code; routes.py launch_redeem) - now at most one live and none on disk, but its scope and lifetime are unchanged; (b) page tokens persisted by shells BEFORE this change stay live server-side after the SPA deletes them from IndexedDB (revocable from Control -> Devices; no automatic revoke here); (c) docs/api.md shows modified with no content diff (line endings only) in that checkout and was not staged. notes: CORRECTION ee6dabb (reviews/T-101-f357434.claude.md CHANGES): the system-principal session write moved from _finish into refuse only (its discarded edge exists only from PAUSED and AWAITING_APPROVAL); _finish takes session_actor=None and otherwise passes the command's actor, so record_end/lost/record_handoff/approval_answered keep headless_session's Core-only guard. Tests pin the audit trail (execution.ended and the move to ENDED_KILLED are the user's, core.estopped names the user's principal, only the session's writes are system) and add: a user stop of a PAUSED execution that ran, stop_mission over one waiting for approval, a user record_end on a running execution refused with the log unchanged. Reviewer observation O1 (checkpoint.derive runs with the user's actor) left as recorded. CORRECTION ee6dabb (reviews/T-101-f357434.claude.md CHANGES): the system-principal session write moved from _finish into refuse only (its discarded edge exists only from PAUSED and AWAITING_APPROVAL); _finish takes session_actor=None and otherwise passes the command's actor, so record_end/lost/record_handoff/approval_answered keep headless_session's Core-only guard. Tests pin the audit trail (execution.ended and the move to ENDED_KILLED are the user's, core.estopped names the user's principal, only the session's writes are system) and add: a user stop of a PAUSED execution that ran, stop_mission over one waiting for approval, a user record_end on a running execution refused with the log unchanged. Reviewer observation O1 (checkpoint.derive runs with the user's actor) left as recorded. REVIEW CHANGES: (1) the system-principal switch is in _finish, shared by every end path (refuse, _ended/record_end, lost, record_handoff, approval_answered), so a user actor calling record_end now succeeds where it was refused - not exploitable, but wider than the bug; move it into refuse only (its discarded transition exists only from PAUSED and AWAITING_APPROVAL) and test that a user record_end is still refused; (2) tests must assert execution.ended and the ENDED_KILLED transition name the user, core.estopped names the user's principal, only the session writes are system; add a user stop of a PAUSED execution and stop_mission. Reviewer: not exploitable; skipping the session on a stop would break D5. FIXING 2026-10-15 (Claude fork). DONE f357434. CAUSE: an AWAITING_APPROVAL (or PAUSED) execution has run (process_seq 1) and has no session yet - executions._finish writes the headless session at its end. A user's stop_execution or e-stop ends it through stop -> refuse -> _finish with the USER's actor, and sessions.headless_session accepts only Core's (_core). FIX at that one place: _finish writes the session as the system principal (queries.system_principal) when the command's actor is not Core; every other row of the transaction keeps the user's actor, and core.estopped is recorded in the same transaction as before. Claude Code adapter (read, not run): archeus/harnesses/claude_code/adapter.py:182,241 keeps the provider session in adapter_state['session']; Execution.session_id is set only by sessions.headless_session (sessions.py:135), so the adapter took the same failing path. Reproduced by the reviewer on the fake harness: the e-stop record transaction fails, the route errors, core.estopped is not recorded; the sentinel and the manager still stop the processes. Safety-relevant (the e-stop's record and its answer to the user are wrong).

QUESTION:
Commits f357434 and ee6dabb only (`git show <sha>`). One question: the authority change. _finish (session end recording) now writes the session as the SYSTEM principal when the command's actor is a user, on the grounds that only Core records what it observed of a session. Is this consistent with the canonical authority/actor model (p20-design-gate.md item 83, D3, D4 and the actor rules they rest on - read them in docs/architecture/ at ee6dabb via `git show ee6dabb:docs/architecture/p20-design-gate.md`), and can a user principal now cause, through this path, any record or transition it could not cause before (e.g. ending or recording a session it does not own, or an event attributed to the system it did not observe)? Say whether the change needs a canonical amendment that is missing.

Finding 3 (LocalNode.spawn notes a SpawnWriteFailed as isolation_failed on the isolated path) not addressed, latent. CORRECTION 438d5ca: a pre-child write failure is charged. _start does its pre-child writes (class dirs, marker, stale tombstone/halt report, prompt) in one block; their OSError becomes base.SpawnWriteFailed (a SpawnFailed subclass) and the manager refuses it with the charged `workspace` stop reason (P11 note 11), so a persistent failure spends max_attempts and the task ends FAILED. A launch's own SpawnFailed stays uncharged `binding` (O7); an OSError the launch raises is still an uncharged refusal, not a crash; any non-OSError propagates. Commit message corrects c91d4f1's "aborted the whole tick" (it stopped the execution thread and Core). Tests: never-writable prompt -> two charged workspace attempts, task FAILED; one-off -> charged once, retry ENDED_OK; launch SpawnFailed stays binding; RuntimeError propagates, nothing refused. OPEN, not this card: a permanent Popen failure (spawn_detached returning None) still re-dispatches uncharged every tick - O7's backoff/breaker; _private_dirs/_isolated_env failures on the isolated path stay uncharged (isolation establishment, O7). REVIEW CHANGES (blocking): a pre-child spawn OSError now ends as uncharged `binding` with no retry bound - a persistent prompt-write failure produced 70 ABANDONED executions in 4 s, task still READY (max_attempts 2); before, it stopped Core and was reconciled LOST (charged). p11 note 11 limits binding to a stale plan or provider terms; owner O7 (2026-10-08) makes only isolation-establishment failures uncharged, with a backoff/breaker not yet built. Fix: charge it as `workspace` (like _workspace and the inventory failure), test a persistent failure ends FAILED, pin the refusal to OSError only (a surviving mutant). Non-blocking: kill does not survive a Core restart (T-105); POSIX tree kill is SIGTERM to the group; tests fake "still running"; commit message overstates. FIXING 2026-10-15 (Claude fork). DONE c91d4f1: (1) manager._orphans(live, disarmed): an orphan still running gets p['since'] on first sight and is killed through node.kill once grace_s has passed (the existing STOPPING grace, Rig 0.3 s, default 10 s - no new constant), at once when disarmed; collected by the next pass (tombstone, mcp.json dropped). (2) base.spawn: an OSError from _start (before any child) goes through the existing _refuse (tombstone + SpawnFailed), so _spawn refuses the execution with stop_reason binding and the tick continues; a post-child OSError is unchanged. test_T099_a_prompt_that_cannot_be_written now asserts the refusal, the tombstone error and the retry reaching ENDED_OK. Pre-existing, found by the T-099 review: _orphans collects only processes that already exited and kills nothing, so a halted process that never exits keeps its config and is never killed by Core. Proposed; Claude takes it after T-101 under the owner rule of 2026-10-15 unless the owner objects (bounded grace value to be named on the card at claim).

scope: Detached checkout D:/archeus-ci2 (HEAD 82c7e2d = pushed branch head, T-095 merged). tests/v1/integration/test_stream_takeover.py::test_a_concurrent_burst_keeps_exactly_the_last_two_registered and the stream registry behind D2 (slice C, F4: a device's newest stream wins at its cap).
refs: CI run 38654736610 job "test (ubuntu-latest, 3.12)": "assert [39388, 39402] == [39378, 39402]" (1 failed, 5455 passed); the previous run 38646019877 passed this job; p20-design-gate.md D2, slice C items 24-31.
accept: cause named - either the product's "last two registered" is not what the test computes under a concurrent burst (a real D2 race: registration order vs the order the cap evicts in), or the test's expectation is computed wrongly; fixed at the cause; reproduced (e.g. a stress loop or a forced interleaving) before the fix and passing after; no retries/longer timeouts without a cause.
verify: RAN 2026-10-15 (Claude fork) in D:/archeus-ci2: repro - the old spy with a random 0-30 ms delay forced between _register's return and its append failed 8 of 10 with CI's shape (e.g. [54527, 54526] == [54525, 54526]); the new record under the same delay 0 of 10; the fixed test unmodified 30 of 30 passed; product mutant (evict mine[-1], the newest) fails the fixed test; test_stream_takeover + test_api_pools + test_gui_shell (-m "not real_process") 30 passed; ruff clean, diff --check clean, no CR; repro file and D:/t104 deleted. NOT RUN: Linux/CI, full tests/v1.
commits: (detached checkout D:/archeus-ci2 on 82c7e2d) 64cf479, dccca94
review: reviews/T-104-64cf479.claude.md APPROVE (independent Claude reviewer, 2026-10-15; finding 1 taken in dccca94)
notes: FOLLOW-UP dccca94 (Claude): the test asserts _Stream is built under the pool lock (mutant building it before the lock fails with that message). PUSHED 2026-10-15 on the owner's answer "Push now (Recommended)": 82c7e2d..dccca94. DONE 64cf479, TEST-ONLY. CAUSE: the test, not D2. Streams._register evicts and appends under one lock in _open order (sse.py:103-112), and the test's _ports reads that real order; but the spy appended to `order` after _register returned, outside the pool's lock, so two neighbours could be recorded swapped while the pool kept the right pair (CI: pool [39388, 39402], recorded [39378, 39402]; the numbers are the clients' ports). Fix: record where _register builds the stream (monkeypatch sse._Stream), inside the pool's lock. D2 is unambiguous here and the product holds it. Process note: the mutant was reverted with `git checkout -- archeus/api/sse.py` in this owned detached checkout (no other change in the file); the README forbids that command and the fork records it.

Review findings 3-5 (Linux /proc starttime, taskkill /T descendants, isolated orphans and R6-A) not addressed. REVIEW CHANGES (blocking): _adopt_orphans is outside boot's per-item failure handling - an unended registry line whose process is alive but whose Execution row is missing (e.g. after a backup restore) or whose harness is not registered raises out of boot(): compaction, MCP sweep and isolation sweep skipped, Core exits 3 on every start while that process lives (probed: AttributeError, booted False, stale mcp.json). Fix: per-line try/except folding into failed, handle a missing row, test. Tests: no-row stray line with a real live process (killed, boot up, sweep ran, logged), unregistered harness (same), a line that raises (Core booted, keys swept, error raised after), and the stranger-pid case (create time no longer matches: neither taken in nor killed). Review findings 3-5 (Linux /proc starttime, taskkill /T descendants, isolated orphans and R6-A) not addressed. Fix: per-line try/except folding into failed, handle a missing row, test. DONE 9f057fc: boot() calls _adopt_orphans(live) before the registry compaction and the MCP sweep: every unended registry spawn whose execution is not live (nor already in _procs) and whose process is alive by identity (pid + create time) goes into _procs as an orphan; the sweep then keeps its mcp.json, and the existing _orphans pass kills it after grace_s (at once when disarmed) and collects it (tombstone, registry end line, config dropped). A process already gone is left to compaction and the sweep as before; a live execution is never taken in a second time. Tests simulate the restart with a really running fake process ended in the DB (X.lost) after the old Core's _procs is cleared. ### T-107 · Idempotency: a replayed re-arm re-arms again; the PIN is recoverable from the stored request hash
owner: claude · status: done
auth: Owner rule 2026-10-15 (implement without waiting for Codex); security defects inside accepted designs (D10, slice I secrets inventory), found by the T-098 independent review.
scope: AS BUILT (detached checkout D:/archeus-p20-idem on d6877a2): archeus/infra/db/writer.py (PROOF_ARGS kept out of request_hash; _one returns (result, replayed), _run sets Future.replayed), archeus/api/server.py (Handler.run exposes req.replayed), archeus/api/routes.py (rearm returns the stored answer on a replay before step_up.reset and clear_estop; decide_approval and the post_message decision path reset the strike count only when the command ran), archeus/infra/db/migrations/0016_idempotency_proofs.sql (drops rows of executions.rearm, Authorization.decide, Authorization.decide_by_message), tests/v1/integration/test_writer.py, test_presence.py (J04-J06), test_db_schema.py (version 16 + the 0016 test). No gate-doc append (another fork owns p20-design-gate.md).
refs: reviews/T-098-d6877a2.claude.md findings 4 and 5; D10; item 122 ("a proof is per request"); T-093/T-103 secrets inventory.
accept: (4) replaying an idempotency key returns the stored answer and performs NO side effect - after a later e-stop, a replayed re-arm leaves Core disarmed; every route checked for side effects done outside the writer on replay. (5) no stored request hash lets a PIN or any step-up proof be recovered: secret fields (step_up and any other proof) are excluded from the stored hash, or the hash is keyed with a Core-held secret - choose the smaller change that keeps conflicting-reuse detection meaningful, and say why; existing rows (kept 24 h) - decide whether to purge rows that hold a step-up-derived hash and do it if the change makes them stale. Tests + mutants for both; the secrets inventory test updated if it lists stored hashes.
verify: RAN 2026-10-15 on e90217a (Claude fork): writer + db_schema 50 passed; test_presence 40 passed; focused set (test_writer, test_db_schema, test_presence, test_estop_drills, test_lifecycle, test_policy, unit test_api_errors, contract test_core_client, unit test_p20_secrets, judge test_g05_estop; -m "not real_process") 349 passed, 2 skipped (a first run on a reused basetemp gave 16 PermissionError setup errors in test_writer from the basetemp clean-up; rerun on a fresh basetemp clean). Mutants on a git-archive copy of e90217a, 6 of 6 caught, baseline and restored 5 passed: M1 rearm ignores replay (J04), M2 step_up hashed again (writer test + J04), M3 decide resets on replay (J05), M4 message decision resets on replay (J06), M5 writer reports no replay (writer test + J04), M6 migration misses Authorization.decide (0016 test). Brute force in the writer test: all 10^6 six-digit PINs hashed the old way against the stored hash, none found (positive control: the old construction reproduces exactly). ruff clean, git diff --check clean, no CR. Temp dirs D:/t107, t107b, t107m deleted. NOT RUN: full tests/v1, POSIX, SPA e2e, real CLI.
commits: (detached checkout D:/archeus-p20-idem on d6877a2) e90217a
review: reviews/T-107-e90217a.claude.md CHANGES (independent Claude reviewer, 2026-10-15; 2 required, 1 doc)
notes: RE-REVIEW APPROVE 2026-10-16: busy-shape probe P1 (503 busy, commit after, same-key retry 200 {armed:false}, Core disarmed, fresh re-arm 200 {armed:true}) and P2 (replay while armed answers armed:true) hold; SPA cannot show armed while disarmed; J07 kills R10/R11/R13. RECORDED, NOT FIXED (Low): (1, recommended) J04 builds only the later-e-stop shape, its docstring overclaims the busy-retry shape - mutant R14 (replay clears the sentinel when no e-stop came after the re-arm) passes every suite; add P1 as a test or narrow the docstring (test_presence.py:773-789); (2, optional) no test replays while armed - R15 (replay always answers armed:false) passes; one assertion in J04. Info: in the busy shape the event log keeps a core.rearmed with no effect (cost of the fail-safe choice; nothing derives armed from it). REVIEW CHANGES - NOT FIXED (owner: finish running agents then stop): (1) a replayed re-arm answers {"armed": true} while Core stays disarmed; and a same-key retry after 503 busy (the SPA sends one by itself) now leaves a committed re-arm unapplied (on d6877a2 it re-armed) - on replay return dict(out, armed=not LocalNode.disarmed()), J04 expects {"armed": False}; decide whether a committed-but-unapplied re-arm should apply on the retry; (2) no test pins that a replayed re-arm leaves the strike count alone, which the PIN exclusion relies on - mutant R10 (reset moved above the replay check) survived all 102 tests and let 12 wrong PINs through with the device ACTIVE; add the re-arm version of J05 (four wrong, a replay of the used key, the fifth wrong revokes); (3 doc) the audit: resume/handoff are guarded on a key replay by the launch-sequence check, not the duplicate flag - two concurrent same-key requests could still launch twice (old code, not probed). What holds: replay signal reliable (concurrent duplicate, restart, both bindings); e-stop replay fails safe; step_up the only proof field; stored hash bound to the device (another device reusing the key gets 400); migration 0016 exact, empty-DB safe, number unique; 10^6 brute-force test real; 102 passed. DONE e90217a. CHOICE for (5): exclude proof fields from the hash rather than HMAC-key it - smaller (no new Core secret to store, inventory and rotate across restarts) and stronger: with the PIN in the hash, a used key answered 409 for a wrong PIN and 200 for the right one, an uncounted PIN oracle for 24 h; excluded, any PIN on a used key is the same replay that runs nothing and spends no attempt; a key reused with any other difference still conflicts. Rows of the three proof-taking commands written before are dropped by 0016 (recoverable PIN; would no longer match a retry; a retry across the upgrade runs the command again). ROUTE AUDIT (every route/service that submits a keyed command): FIXED rearm (clear_estop + step_up.reset skipped on replay), decide_approval and post_message decision (step_up.reset skipped on replay). SAFE, unchanged: estop - the sentinel is engaged before the writer by design (item 83, sentinel first); a replay re-engages it, which is the fail-safe direction, Core's armed state is the sentinel itself and rearm has no state precondition so it can always be re-armed; skipping it on a key match would let a client that wrongly reuses a key stop nothing; revoke_device - close_device on a replay closes streams of an already-revoked device (no-op); sessions launch/resume/handoff (core/sessions/service.py) - _launch is guarded by the session's launched_seq/launch_seq and resume/handoff by out['duplicate'], so a replay opens no terminal; register_account/set_account_state - the probe runs before the writer and is a read; import_meeting/ingest.import_file - stores a content-addressed blob before the writer (idempotent); knowledge moves, forget, feedback and all other routes - req.run only. OUTSIDE SCOPE (noted, not changed): register_account/set_account_state put the live probe answer into the hashed arguments, so a retry whose probe answer changed (e.g. login state flipped) gets 409 instead of the stored answer; the T-103 runtime secrets gate (other checkout) should count the user-chosen PIN among the values it scans for. Found by the T-098 review: same key + same PIN after a later e-stop returned 200 and re-armed (no new core.rearmed event: the writer replays its answer and the route clears the sentinel anyway); the 6-digit PIN was recovered from the plain sha256 request hash in 3.0 s while the device PIN record uses PBKDF2 200k; approval decisions with a step-up already wrote such rows.

## Recovery after the 2026-10-19 wipe (post-wipe notes)

## What happened and what was recovered

The `D:\Claude` working tree (git object store, `.collab`, the owner's uncommitted docs) was
deleted 2026-10-19 ~12:33 UTC. Cause: a Qwen `pi` run cleaning its T-111 test fixture ran
`cmd /c rmdir /s /q D:\t111` from a bash heredoc; the heredoc turned `\t` into a TAB, so cmd
received `rmdir /s /q D:` = the current directory on drive D, which was the repo root. The
archeus GUI then wrote a fresh project scaffold over the empty folder (it did NOT cause the
deletion — Qwen's own session log holds the command).

Recovery:
- Canonical repo re-cloned to `C:\Users\mab\archeus-recovery\repo` (origin `c8f5bd5`, all pushed
  work intact).
- Unpushed commits rebuilt from the surviving sibling worktrees (content identical; original SHAs
  dead) and pushed to origin under `recover/*`:
  - `recover/p20-p21-chain` = cddec29 → T-112/T-113 (e9c2191) → T-116 (3dba2cc) → P21 gate (d0b98d3)
  - `recover/p21-a`, `recover/p21-c`, `recover/p21-d`, `recover/p21-fg` — the four P21 slices, each
    off the recovered gate, NOT yet reviewed or merged.
- Lost and not recoverable: `.collab` history (board, all review files, the T-110 O7 note), the
  owner's uncommitted `docs/design/ARCHEUS_V2_*` edits and untracked files in `D:\Claude`. Check
  Windows Previous Versions / File History on `D:\Claude` for those.

Qwen is now contained (`.collab/qwen_run.py`): cwd locked to `C:\Users\mab\qwen-sandbox` (an
isolated worktree), analysis/review cards run with NO bash tool, test-running cards refused
without explicit owner sign-off.

  (`0017_artifact_records.sql` vs `0017_metrics_daily.sql`). Renumber one (A=0017, D=0018) when
  merging.

  four slices are reviewed — same round-5 merge discipline.
- `.collab` must never again be the only copy of state: this board now lives in the tracked repo
  under `.collab/` of the recovery clone, but confirm the repo's `.gitignore` is not excluding it
  before relying on commits.

## Hard rules (unchanged, plus the wipe lesson)

- Never build a shell deletion path by string-interpolation or through a heredoc: `\t`, `\n` etc.
  are mangled. Delete only with Python `shutil.rmtree` on an absolute path asserted to be under a
  known temp root. Never `cmd /c rmdir`, never a bare `D:`/`C:` drive spec.
- Qwen never runs in or cd's to a real repo; its cwd is the sandbox worktree only.
- `.collab` and any state that matters is kept in a tracked, pushed location — not an ignored dir
  that one `rmdir` erases.
